Add Global Semiconductors, remove HSTECH 
We initiate d an Opportunistic Idea on Global 
Semiconductors on 9 Sep t 2026. We expect sustained AI 
investments and rising AI adoption to support earnings 
growth, with demand widening beyond processors to memory, 
networking, and advanced manufacturing equipment . 
Valuations have also eased since the middle of the year. 
We remove Hang Seng Technology (31 Oct 2024 to 24 Sep 
2026: -1.2%) amid mixed earnings trends, while AI 
monetisation has yet to keep pace with rising investments. 
Widening US-China tech restrictions adds to the uncertainty, 
and we see better risk-adjusted opportunities elsewhere.

Ongoing Opportunistic Ideas 
Euro area banks: A higher-for-longer rates backdrop should 
extend the earnings upcycle, with further ECB tightening  
expected. Corporate loan demand is showing recovery, while 
non-interest income  is growing . Strong capital generation 
supports higher dividends and continued share buybacks. 
Global Power & Electrification: Rising AI and data centre 
power demand should drive a multi -year grid and 
electrification investment cycle. Power availability and grid 
capacity are emerging bottlenecks to the AI infra buildout. 
Addressing these constraints requires capex and time, which 
supports strong earnings visibility for grid equipment makers. 
Japan Banks : BoJ policy normali sation drives net interest 
margin upside, while Japan’s reflation cycle supports nominal 
loan demand, corporate capex and wealth activity. 
Governance reform is a re-rating driver  independent of the 
rate cycle. This includes recycling legacy holdings towards 
growth, buybacks and dividends.  
MSCI Taiwan: Taiwan’s foundry leadership makes it a critical 
supplier to global tech firms, while its broadening AI hardware 
supply chain is driving strong revenue growth.  
Global High Dividend  payers: Provides a higher -income 
equity allocation with historically lower volatility than the MSCI 
ACWI and modest downside cushioning on balance.  
US Communication Services: We remain constructive and 
expect further upward earnings revisions, driven by improving 
digital ad-spending and AI monetisation. 
Sector views: Continue to broaden & diversify 
We upgrade US Materials to Overweight as robust capex 
drives demand for industrial metals and construction 
materials, while we remain bullish on gold . US Technology 
and Communication Services remain the primary growth 
engines, supported by structural AI tailwinds. We remain 
Overweight in Financials across the US, Europe ex -UK, and 
Japan, underpinned by earnings stability  and elevated rates 
supporting interest income. This is further supported by a US 
capital markets recovery  and European balance -sheet 
strength. We remain Overweight in Europe ex-UK Industrials, 
reflecting EU fiscal stimulus and Germany's infrastructure 
acceleration legislation. 
In China, we broaden our Overweight exposure by upgrading 
three sectors: Materials (benefiting from structurally elevated 
commodity prices), Healthcare (biotech innovation and a robust 
pipeline sustaining growth), and Financials (easing margin 
pressure, fee recovery, and capital market reform). Our existing 
Overweight positio ns in Technology and Communication 
Services maintain exposure to China's AI build-out, with fiscal 
deployment into high-tech infrastructure serving as a near-term 
catalyst. On the other hand, we downgrade Utilities to 
Underweight on capped margins and retain our Underweight 
views on Real Estate and Consumer Staples.

Our view

• We maintain our Overweight on gold, and our 3 - and 12-month price targets at USD 4,750/oz and 5,000/oz, respectively.

Gold outlook:  Gold has remained resilient despite a more 
hawkish Fed and higher real yields, suggesting much of the 
tightening is already reflected in prices. With markets 
discounting an aggressive Fed path, we see an increasingly 
asymmetric risk -reward: limited downsid e risk from further 
rate repricing, but meaningful upside should Fed rate 
expectations shift towards being less restrictive. Official -
sector demand has slowed from recent years but has become 
broader based, while investor positioning remains well below 
levels seen at the start of the 2022 hiking cycle. 
Oil outlook: Oil prices have risen sharply as recent attacks 
cast greater uncertainty over Saudi export s, even as Gulf 
crude flows overall remain resilient. Other regional producers 
have helped sustain exports, containing the immediate supply 
impact. However, adequate US commercial crude stocks 
mask thinner buffers elsewhere, with strategic reserves and 
fuel inventories low. This leaves the m arket more vulnerable 
to a prolonged disruption, supporting a higher geopolitical risk 
premium and an upward revision to our outlook.

15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA

### Page 19

Global Market Outlook 19

USD view

Further Fed tightening supports the USD near term. We expect the USD to remain supported, with our 3-month DXY forecast 
at 100.2. Resilient US payrolls for August alongside sticky energy and IT price -driven inflation reinforce the Fed's focus on 
containing inflation. Following its 25bp hike to 3.75–4.00%, we expect another hike by end-2026 and potentially another one in 
early H1 2027. Further tightening should keep US front -end yields elevated and support the USD. However, money markets 
already price a more aggressive Fed hiking path than our expectations, limiting the scope for further USD gains and leaving the 
USD vulnerable if incoming data weaken the case for additional hikes. 
Fading inflation and narrowing rate advantage point to a softer USD in the medium term. We expect the USD index (DXY) 
to decline gradually towards 98 over 12 months. Inflation pressures are expected to moderate  as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs fade, reducing the need for continued Fed tightening. We forecast that the Fed funds rate may peak in H1 2027  in the 
current mini-rate hike cycle and then gradually decline thereafter as inflation moves closer towards target , supporting our DXY 
call. Further ECB and BoJ tightening should also progressively narrow the USD's relative rate advantage, supporting gradual 
USD moderation. Resilient US growth and elevated government bond yields should, however, limit the pace of depreciation.

Near-term EUR weakness; constructive medium term. The ECB raised its deposit 
rate 25bps to 2.50% in September. Potential ECB tightening should narrow the US-
Euro area rate differential over time, supporting EUR over 12 months.

GBP/USD 1.3219 1.33 1.35 
Rangebound with near term downside risk: UK inflation rose to 3.1% y/y, keeping 
BoE tightening risk alive, but softer labour conditions should limit the extent of 
tightening. Fed -BoE policy divergence may constrain GBP near term, while an 
expected peak in US rates by mid -2027 should allow a modes t recovery over the 
medium term. The Autum Budget in October could increase volatility.

USD/JPY 158.86 157 153 
Constructive JPY. The BoJ's September 25bp hike to 1.25% reinforces policy 
normalisation. The still wide  US-Japan yield differential may limit near-term JPY 
gains, but further BoJ tightening should gradually narrow the gap and support JPY 
over the medium term towards our target of 153.

AUD/USD 0.7012 0.73 0.74 
Near-term AUD weakness; constructive medium term: Australia’s Q2 GDP grew 
0.4% q/q, reinforcing domestic resilience. A relatively restrictive RBA stance supports 
AUD, while eventual narrowing in relative rate differentials should improve the 
medium-term outlook.

NZD/USD 0.5662 0.56 0.57 
Rangebound: The RBNZ raised rates 25bps to 2.75%, while New Zealand’s Q2 
GDP grew 0.2% q/q. Improving growth provides support, but an uneven recovery and 
near-term USD strength should limit NZD gains.

USD/CAD 1.4138 1.39 1.37 
Near-term CAD weakness; modest medium-term recovery: Canada employment 
fell 41,700 in August vs. a 15,000 expected rise, while the BoC held rates at 2.25%. 
Softer labour conditions and Fed-BoC divergence may constrain CAD near term.

USD/CHF 0.8278 0.82 0.79 
Near-term CHF weakness; medium-term CHF recovery: Swiss inflation surprised 
higher at 0.8% y/y, but the wide Fed -SNB rate differential should continue to favour 
USD near term. As Fed tightening peaks and the US rate advantage narrows, CHF 
should gradually recover over 12 months.

USD/CNH 6.7159 6.70 6.67 
Gradual CNH appreciation: China’s industrial output in August rose 5.2% y/y, 
beating estimates, but retail sales growth slowed to 0.4% on weak domestic demand. 
Production resilience and PBoC currency support favour modest CNH appreciation, 
while soft consumption and an easing policy bias should limit the pace of gains.

USD/SGD 1.2794 1.28 1.26 
Rangebound near term; constructive SGD medium term: Further Fed tightening 
and elevated US yields may limit SGD appreciation near term. Resilient 
fundamentals and MAS's exchange -rate policy should support gradual SGD 
appreciation as US rate support fades.

USD/INR 95.96 – 98 
Modestly bearish INR: India’s crude oil imports rose 26% y/y in August, and a USD 
77bn AI import deficit are both adding to structural import pressures. Higher -for-
longer US rates remain a headwind, while capital inflows and RBI intervention should 
smooth out near-term volatility and support a gradual INR depreciation towards 98.

USD/MYR 4.0865 – 4.00 Modestly bullish MYR: Resilient domestic demand, strong exports and corporate FX conversion support MYR; external uncertainty should keep appreciation gradual.

USD/KRW 1,368.9 – 1,540 
Bearish KRW: Recent KRW -supportive corporate USD selling is fading, while 
reduced National Pension Service’s FX hedging and prospect of USD 350bn US 
investment package by the South Korean national investment committee could 
increase USD demand. Higher US rates add a he adwind, supporting KRW 
depreciation over 12 months.

### Page 21

Global Market Outlook 21

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 22

Global Market Outlook 22 
Fig. 36 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Source: Standard Chartered

### Page 27

Global Market Outlook 27

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well -diversified, long -term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family. 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever 
Approach 
Planning for Today 
Requires ensuring liquidity and income 
flows take centre stage 
Securing Tomorrow 
Entails a well-diversified investment and 
protection portfolio with a focus on growth, 
ensuring inflation is accounted for and risks 
are mitigated 
Building for Forever 
Involves greater focus on long-term returns 
given the time horizon of your portfolio can 
be measured in decades, and might also 
include business interests, real estate, 
collectibles or charitable funds

SC Wealth Select  
 Managing your wealth through the decades – Today, Tomorrow and Forever

### Page 28

Global Market Outlook 28

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

Advisory 
Process 
Following a 
holistic 
approach to 
managing  
your wealth 
We follow a rigorous process to ensure your 
needs and objectives are well -understood, and 
your portfolio is well-aligned and manage s to  
deliver on these objectives. 
However, markets constantly evolve and your 
needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic reb alancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026ECONEWS

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Stronger trade posture pays off. The EU and China have agreed to
restrain hybrid cars exports to the EU to a level half or less than cur -
rently projected over the next four years, cutting exports by “several
million units” though still allowing them to rise. China also agreed to

drop its tariffs on EUR 4 bn of EU exports and provide easier access to
Chinese rare earths and magnets under a fast-track licensing sche -
me. These announcements were made at the conclusion of EU Trade
Commissioner Maroš Šefcovic’s visit to Beijing, following a recent es -
calation in trade tensions. Meanwhile, France and Germany released
a joint non-paper on Strengthening European resilience, addressing
global macroeconomic imbalances and market distorting practices

calling notably for:
i) Broadening the tools the European Commission may use to address
unfair trade practices (including undervalued exchange rates) or im -
port surges;
ii) Procedural changes to make them easier to use (qualified majority
of member states needed to block rather than authorise them). These
measures will be discussed during the next EU council on October
15-16th.
The European Commission also unveiled a new critical materials pac -

kage involving 46 strategic extraction, processing or recycling projects
spread across 16 member states (including 5 in France). The goal is to
reduce the EU’s dependence on China for critical metals.
EUROZONE
Price pressures are broadening, activity holds up. Producer price in -
flation accelerated further in August (8.3% y/y 1 from 5.8%), driven by
energy. Pressures are becoming more widespread, notably in capital
goods and above all intermediate goods (6.8%). They are partly offset

by non-durable consumer goods, which remain in deflation (-0.8%).
Given the PPI's close correlation with the HICP , this points to further
upside risks to consumer inflation. On the activity front, final September
PMIs confirmed the flash estimates, with the composite index still solid
at 53.1 and the manufacturing index stable at 52.7. The construction
PMI improved but remains depressed (43.4; +0.4 pt). Retail sales edged
up 0.1% m/m2 in August.

France
Modest spread relief as budget positioning starts. The spread between
French and German 10Y yields narrowed to 136 bps 3 on October 9
(mostly thanks to a decline in the French yield). As the Finance Com -
mittee of the National Assembly started discussing the 2027 budget,
the government reiterated its strong intent not to deviate from the
5% of GDP deficit target and to get a budget adopted in time, likely
without a vote. This is the most likely outcome — but not to be im -

plemented before 10 December (given mandatory timelines) — which
effectively removes any incentive for MPs to back unpopular measures
during the process. We can therefore expect heated debates (including
multiple no-confidence votes being tabled but rejected) and a lack in
fiscal discipline. In parallel, the "Rassemblement National" party ("Na-
tional Rally" in English) presented its fiscal strategy, which includes an
alternative budget for 2027 and an aggressive fiscal consolidation path

throughout 2032. The party called for a referendum to constitutionalise
1 Year-on-year
2 Month-on-month
3 Basis points
a “golden rule” that would require a deficit at least 0.5 pp below the
debt-stabilising threshold (roughly 3% of GDP), as long as the public
debt ratio exceeds 60% of GDP . The golden rule would require a budget
balance adjustment of 0.5 pp per year if the fiscal deficit is more than
1 pp above the stabilising threshold. The adjustment measures put

forward lack specifics but likely overstate savings, understate costs
and rely on overly optimistic growth assumptions (despite large fiscal
consolidation, at least 1.7% annually over the next three years — a
pace reached only 4 times in France since 2008, apart from post-pan-
demic reopening, and well in excess of the roughly 1% growth potential
estimated by most international institutions). That said, it is significant
that this party has chosen to fly the flag for fiscal discipline loud and

clear. Other parties should do the same.
Activity is holding up. New vehicle registrations rose 11% y/y in Sep -
tember, confirming a marked acceleration over the past three months
(+9.4% y/y in Q3). In Q3, growth came entirely from electric vehicles
(+112% y/y), whose share of registrations reached an all-time high
(38% in Q3, 41% in September). Export growth continued in August,
both in services (+5.2% y/y YTD) and in goods (+5.6% y/y YTD) — though

driven by prices rather than volume in the latter case. Manufacturing
output posted only a moderate rebound in August (+0.3% m/m; fol -
lowing cumulative declines of 2.8% over the previous three months),
limited by renewed supply constraints in Q3 (which limited produc -
tion at 31.5% of companies; +5 pp compared to H1). Industrial produc-
tion fell (-0.3% m/m) as a result of a decline in electricity production
(-2.4% m/m) driven by the heatwave. Credit growth remains dynamic.

Bank and market financing to NFCs saw steady growth in August (+4%).
New housing loans rebounded slightly in August (EUR 12.2 bn) fol -
lowing a relatively weak July, whilst interest rates continued to rise.
Consumer credit remained buoyant.
Upcoming: Banque de France monthly Business Survey (Monday), Bu-
siness creations in September (Friday).
Germany
Bullish official growth forecast upgrade despite mixed data. The
German government raised its 2026 GDP growth forecast to 1.3%

— the fastest pace since 2017 — from a prior estimate of 0.5%, citing
stronger-than-expected exports and a surge in government spending.
The 2027 forecast was also raised to 1.1% from 0.9%. Industrial pro -
duction was stronger than expected in August (+2.3% y/y versus -1.7%
in July; the highest level since March 2025 and higher than antici -
pated), driven mainly by strong growth in construction output, prima -
rily in public works (+10.5%; to the highest level since November 2021).

By contrast, manufacturing output fell by -0.2% (after +3.3% in July)
due to a decline in transport equipment production (lowest level since
April 2022). Likewise, new factory orders hit their lowest level in one
year in August (-10.6% m/m). However, this decline is mainly due
to a base effect. Export growth remains solid: +4.4% YTD, despite a
-0.8% m/m decline in August. Exports to the Eurozone continue to rise
(+6.3% y/y YTD), while exports to the U.S. have returned to positive

territory (+1.1%) and those to China continue to decline (-12.9%). The
trade surplus reached EUR 19.5 bn in August (+4% y/y YTD, despite a
-9.8% m/m decline).

ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
UNITED STATES
Mild slowdown in services and more rate hikes signaled. The non-ma-
nufacturing ISM survey fell 0.5 pp4 to 54.9 in September due to a sharp
slowdown in the business activity component (down 5.2 pp to 56.5 pp,
still a robust expansion). Employment rose and supply pressures in -
tensified for the first time since June, while the price gauge reached

its highest level (74, up 1.4 pp) since 2022. Meanwhile, the minutes
of the September FOMC meeting confirmed the Fed’s hawkish outlook
and noted that the AI-buildout carried growing weight in the reading of
current inflation data and outlook. Governor Waller and St. Louis Fed
President Musalem mentioned the need for additional rate hikes while
remaining flexible on timing. The New York Fed survey of consumer in-
flation expectations showed them rising to 3.9%. We continue to expect

two more hikes, the next one coming in December.
Upcoming: September CPI (Wednesday), September PPI and Retail
Sales (Thursday), September Industrial Production (Friday).
JAPAN
Solid wage growth fails to lift household spending. Total wage growth
eased to 3.8% y/y in August (down 0.5 pp) as bonus payments stalled
(0% y/y, after 5.3% y/y in July). This allowed continued real wage growth
at 1.5% y/y, extending an uninterrupted run of positive readings in 2026

as administrative measures kept inflation in check. However, this did
not prevent real household spending from contracting in August for the
ninth consecutive month (-3.1% y/y).
EMERGING MARKETS
The IIF reports that nonresident portfolio flows to EMs turned negative
at -USD 26.3 bn in September, after USD 11.4 bn in August, the first net
outflow since June, as a hawkish Fed lifted US real yields and the USD.
Equity outflows reached -USD 19.2 bn, led by heavy foreign selling of

Korean equities, while debt flows turned negative at -USD 7.0 bn as
hard currency demand faded and EM credit spreads widened late in
the month. Further hikes from DM central banks will create additional
headwinds, but EM should remain resilient as long as global growth
persists.
ASIA
China
Yuan policy defence. Coinciding with the Beijing visit of the EU trade
commissioner, the People's Bank of China issued an unusually lengthy
formal statement rejecting claims that the yuan is undervalued and

defending its exchange-rate management by reiterating that it has a
‘floating exchange rate regime’ with reference to a basket of currencies.
It further argued that China's trade surpluses are driven by rising in -
dustrial competitiveness rather than currency under-devaluation and
that exchange-rate adjustments will not resolve structural global im -
balances. Nonetheless, it allowed the yuan to hit a four-year high vs.
the CFETS basket — bringing its year-to-date gain to 5.7%. The PBOC

set the USD/CNY's reference rate at 6.7330, the strongest level since
2023.
India
Monetary tightening. The RBI raised its repo rate by 25bp to 5.5%, its
first hike since February 2023, and shifted to a “calibrated tightening”
stance amidst rising inflationary pressures. The RBI introduced new
measures to stabilise the rupee, including direct dollar supplies to
state-owned oil companies and tighter restrictions on FX derivatives.
4 Percentage points

EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST, AFRICA
Central Europe
Cautious monetary policy stance amid mild inflation uptick. Poland’s
and Romania’s Central banks kept policy rates unchanged at 3.75% and
6.5%, respectively. Inflation accelerated in September, with a mild up-
tick in the Czech Republic and Hungary (respectively 2.5% and 1.6% y/y)
and a more entrenched increase in Poland (4% y/y). Inflation is now
above the official target in the Czech Rep. and Poland, but fuel price

caps in both countries should contain inflation dynamics.
Gulf
PMIs for September point for a firm but uneven non-oil recovery. Both
Saudi and UAE PMIs stood at 55.3, driven mostly by export orders in
the UAE and by domestic demand in Saudi Arabia. On the contrary,
Qatar’s PMI fell again (to 46.7), while Kuwait’s PMI also declined but
remained in expansionary zone (at 52.4).
Türkiye
Manufacturing production contracted by 1.6% m/m in August, the lar-

gest monthly decrease since the beginning of the year. On a y/y ba -
sis, it fell by 2.3%. Both domestic demand and exports seem to have
contributed negatively to this poor outcome. On a positive note, in
September, CPI inflation (1.9% m/m, 27.9% y/y) slowed more than the
market expected, and exports rebounded.
LATIN AMERICA
Inflationary pressures slightly up across the region. In Brazil, headline
CPI inflation accelerated to 4.6% y/y from 4.2% y/y in August (above the

central bank's 4.5% target), partly reflecting the reversal of a tempora-
ry electricity price discount in August. In Mexico, inflation rose to 3.5%
in September from 3.3% in August, while in Colombia it reached 6.3%,
slightly up from 6.2% in August. Inflation remained high in Chile but
was stable at 4.1%. In Peru, it was also slightly up (4.2% vs. 4.1%); the
central bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 4.25% for the thirteenth
consecutive month but maintained an upward bias amid rising inflation

and energy prices.
COMMODITIES
Energy: Limited relief. Continuation of skirmishes in the Gulf of Hormuz
and escalation of the conflict between the Houthis and a Saudi-led
coalition left oil prices fluctuating around $100/b for Brent. However,
the complex security situation has been driving the cost of shipping to
record high levels and the price of physical barrel to early April levels.
Late on Friday, President Trump announced an agreement with Russian

President Putin to release to global markets 4.8 million tonnes of die -
sel (roughly 8 days of European consumption), provided conditions in
refineries allow for it, of which 300,000 tonnes immediately. The White
House also announced a suspension of sanctions on Russia’s exports
of diesel until April 2027. Energy experts expect a de minimis price
impact.
European gas prices (TTF) rose by 11.5% over the week to 82 €/MWh.
EU gas inventories reached 73.6% of full capacities on October 11 th

(the official target is 80% at end October).
Food inflation rose sharply in September. The FAO’s Food Price Index
grew by 5.8% y/y, up from just 3% in August. This was driven by cereal
(+17% y/y) and sugar prices (+15%).

Document 227: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.

Document 357: “假扮受害者”“以骗制骗”！把“反诈”做成一门生意，这家“奇葩”公司把骗子整不会了

Source PDF: all report pdf/“假扮受害者”“以骗制骗”！把“反诈”做成一门生意，这家“奇葩”公司把骗子整不会了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

澳⼤利亚创业公司 Apate.ai ，最近把⼀⻔有点 “ 缺德 ” 的⽣意做出了名堂 —— 派出⼀群  AI ，
假扮受害者，专⻔陪骗⼦聊天。在 “ 消耗骗⼦时间 ” 的同时，公司提取银⾏账号、钱包地址等
关键情报。该公司已获 815 万美元种⼦轮融资，并与澳⼤利亚联邦银⾏等合作，将情报近实
时接⼊机构防线，实现源头反诈。
摘要：
250 万次对话，硬⽣⽣耗掉骗⼦ 17 年的 “ ⻘春 ” 。
澳⼤利亚创业公司  Apate.ai ，最近把⼀⻔有点 “ 缺德 ” 的⽣意做出了名堂 ——派出⼀群  AI ，假扮
受害者，专⻔陪骗⼦聊天。缺德是缺德了点，但缺得⼤快⼈⼼。
诈骗电话，它接；可疑消息，它回。对⽅忙着解释怎么转账，它忙着收集收款账号。骗⼦以为
终于碰上了⼀个有耐⼼的客⼾，聊到最后，⾃⼰的银⾏账⼾、加密货币钱包和假⽹站，却可能
先被送进反诈系统。
这套 “ 以骗制骗 ” 的业务，已经拿到 815 万美元种⼦轮融资。澳⼤利亚联邦银⾏、 TPG Telecom 与
其开展合作，电信分析商  Mobileum 则准备把这项能⼒带向更多国家的运营商。

按公司向 TechCrunch 披露的数据，其 AI 已完成约 250 万次对话，累计消耗诈骗者相当于 17 年的
时间。⽹友看得解⽓，银⾏也确实有理由感兴趣。不过，银⾏愿意掏钱的原因，要从那些看似
没完没了的闲聊⾥找。
陪骗⼦把戏演下去
诈骗者通常需要⼀点耐⼼。
要让⼀个陌⽣⼈相信⾃⼰的⾝份，再按指⽰操作电脑、打开⽹站，最后把钱转出去，中间少不
了解释、催促和反复确认。
Apate 就在这个过程⾥做⽂章。
据《卫报》 2024 年的报道，⼀个名叫  Malcolm 、操英国⼝⾳的⽼年机器⼈，反问诈骗者： “ 你说
的这是什么业务？ ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

这类问题，很适合让骗⼦接着往下讲。毕竟，听不明⽩，不代表骗不成。只要电话还没挂，对
⽅还愿意听，诈骗者就有理由再耐⼼⼀点，再解释⼀遍。好不容易找到⼀个肯配合的 “ 意向客
⼾ ” ，总不能轻易放弃。
Apate 的机器⼈，就是要把这份希望留住。
这些虚拟⼈物有不同的年龄、性别、语⽓和⽂化背景，还会使⽤澳⼤利亚俚语和幽默。它们通
过语⾳和⽂字与诈骗者交流，努⼒让对⽅相信，⾃⼰正在和⼀个真实的⼈说话。
随着话术推进，诈骗者迟早得交代⼀些具体东西。要求转账，就得给收款去向；要求登录⽹
站，就得发链接；冒充银⾏或政府部⻔，也会暴露正在使⽤的⾝份和套路。
公司接触骗⼦的渠道有两种：运营商将识别出的⾼⻛险来电直接转接，或在通信⽹络中设置专
⻔的 " 蜜罐 " 号码，供诈骗者主动拨打和发送消息。随着话术推进，骗⼦为促成转账或登录操作，
必然会提供收款账号、钱包地址或恶意⽹址。 Apate 从对话中提取这些信息，通过旗下产品
Apate Voice 、 Apate Text 和 Apate Insights 交付给机构客⼾。

GSMA 发布的 TPG 合作案例显⽰，试点期间共转接超过 28 万通诈骗电话，累计消耗诈骗者逾 100
天时间，单次对话最⻓ 54 分钟，报告的诈骗流量检测准确率为 98% 。
骗⼦以为⾃⼰正在⼀步步接近受害者的钱包，机器⼈却在⼀点点拼出诈骗活动的轮廓。

### Page 3

点击收听： AI vs. 对亚⻢逊账⼾安全及借记卡的诈骗
骗⼦贡献素材，银⾏采购情报
这⻔⽣意有⼀个很特别的安排：和产品交流的⼈，通常不会付钱；真正付钱的⼈，关⼼的是交
流之后留下了什么。
投资⽅  Investible 的说明显⽰， Apate 通过软件平台和实时告警，为⾦融机构、电信企业及政府
部⻔提供诈骗情报，已经与联邦银⾏、 TPG Telecom 等机构建⽴商业合作。普通⼈不⽤下载⼀
个 “ 帮我⽓骗⼦ ” 的应⽤， Apate 也没有⼿机  App ，其技术主要接⼊机构系统和通信⽹络。
银⾏愿意采购，核⼼逻辑在于时间差。等客⼾已经完成转账再追查资⾦，往往⼗分被动。若机
器⼈在此前的对话中已拿到相关账号或⽹址，银⾏便多了⼀次提前核验和⼲预的机会。联邦银
⾏确认， Apate 获取的情报会被近实时地⽤于其反诈控制系统，帮助发现新趋势、加强检测，
并⽀持跨机构协作。
监管压⼒同样影响机构的采购意愿。英国⾃ 2024 年 10 ⽉起对符合条件的授权推送⽀付诈骗实施
强制赔付安排，澳⼤利亚反诈框架的⼤部分义务将于 2027 年 3 ⽉ 31 ⽇起适⽤，覆盖银⾏、电信企

业和数字平台。诈骗损失由谁承担，谁就更有理由为预防投⼊资⾦。
联邦银⾏ 2025 财年⽤于防范欺诈、诈骗、⾦融犯罪和⽹络威胁的总投⼊超过 9 亿澳元。联邦银⾏
还披露，客⼾诈骗损失在 2025 财年下半年较 2023 财年上半年峰值下降 76%—— 但该⾏同期实施
了多项防护措施，这⼀降幅并不能单独归因于 Apate 。
图⽚来源： Apate
⼀个爸爸的 44 分钟，最后被做成了产品
Apate 的起点，是⼀场被电话打断的家庭野餐。

### Page 4

按照创始⼈兼⾸席执⾏官  Dali Kaafar 的⾃述，⼏年前，他和家⼈在⼀起时接到陌⽣来电。对⽅
冒充⼀家知名科技公司，开始⾏骗。
Kaafar 决定配合⼀下，给孩⼦们找点乐⼦。
他故意与诈骗者周旋，让电话聊了 44 分钟。孩⼦们笑得很开⼼，他也意识到，这 44 分钟⾄少让
骗⼦没空诈骗其他⼈。
随后，他想到另⼀件事：⾃⼰的 44 分钟，也没了。逗⼀次骗⼦，可以算娱乐。每天都这么⼲，
就很难分清究竟是谁耽误了谁。
他由此想到，能否让⼤量不知疲倦的机器⼈代劳，并在聊天时拿到有⽤的信息？
这个念头后来进⼊⻨考瑞⼤学的研究项⽬。⼤学 2023 年的介绍显⽰，团队分析诈骗中的社会⼯
程⼿法，利⽤诈骗通话、邮件⽂本和社交平台聊天记录训练机器⼈，让它们能维持⼈物⾝份，
作出可信回应。
系统取名  Apate ，来⾃希腊神话中的欺骗⼥神。
⼀家反诈公司，请 “ 欺骗⼥神 ” 当招牌，倒也贴合它的⼯作⽅式。
2024 年， Apate 从⼤学拆分出来。同年 8 ⽉，⼤学、公司与联邦银⾏宣布合作试点。 2025 年，

公司⼜获得⼀轮 250 万澳元融资，由  OIF Ventures 领投， Investible 参与。
今年 9 ⽉公布的 815 万美元种⼦轮，则由  Lobby Capital 领投。公司表⽰，将继续开发产品、扩
⼤国际业务，计划设⽴伦敦办公室，并加强美国市场布局。
与此同时， Mobileum 将⾃⼰的⽹络分析平台与  Apate 的对话式  AI 结合。 ARN 报道称，相关能
⼒将以 “Scam Intelligent Defense” 的名称提供给通信服务商。
对⼀家创业公司来说，这是扩⼤市场的⼀条现实路径：借助已有供应商的渠道和系统，接触更
多运营商。
在现有公开材料中， Apate 尚未披露收⼊、合同价格或⽑利率。这个故事已经⾜够让投资⼈下
注，经营成绩还需要更多数字来说明。
最后，两边可能都在⽤ AI“ 加班 ”
在 X 上，有⽹友给这家公司起了个外号：“ 缅北严⽗。 ”

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
还有⼈想到，诈骗者也可以⽤模型驱动⼤量  AI ，最后 “ 演变成了⼀场代理⼈战争 ” 。
对  Apate ⽽⾔，情报业务的重要性会进⼀步上升。只要诈骗活动仍需要收款账⼾、钱包、⽹址
和传播渠道，识别这些线索就仍有价值。公司需要证明，⾃⼰取得的信息⾜够新、⾜够准，⽽
且能帮助客⼾采取⾏动。
Kaafar 早在 2023 年就谈过两边都是机器⼈的可能。他表⽰，如果诈骗机器⼈最终都在与反诈机
器⼈交谈，真实的⼈因此免受损失，他愿意把这看作⼀种胜利。

住宅投資 横ばい圏で推移している
公共投資 高めの水準となっている
項目【今月のポイント】
 東海経済は、持ち直している
 6月の生産（東海3県、季節調整値）は前月
比－7.3％と3ヵ月ぶりに減少。5月まで急
増していた電子部品・デバイスの反動減が
主因であり、自動車関連の弱さも重なった
が、一時的な調整とみられる
 輸出は総じてみると足踏み状態。先行きは
持ち直しを見込むが、米・イランの対立長
期化や熊本地震の影響などで足踏みとな
るリスクがある
 7月の小売販売額は名目で2ヵ月ぶりに前
年比プラスとなったが、実質では2ヵ月連
続でマイナス。一方で、新車販売台数は
4ヵ月連続で前年比プラスとなるなど、個人

消費全体では持ち直しを維持している

生産
6月の鉱工業生産（東海3県）は前月比－7.3％と3ヵ月ぶりに減少した。電子部品・デバイス、輸送機械が減
少し全体を押し下げたが、5月までの大幅上昇の反動が大きく、生産水準はなお高いことから、基調としては
増加している。先行きについては高水準での推移を見込むが、米・イランの対立長期化や熊本地震の影響な
どで下振れるリスクもある。

窯業・土石、金属製品、自動車等が悪化し、全体でも悪化。非製造業では建設が改善する一方、電気・ガスが
悪化し、全体では横ばいとなった。先行き（9月）については、製造業、非製造業とも悪化が見込まれている。

7月の公共工事請負金額（年度累計）は、前年比

Document 421: フィリップス曲線からみるユーロ圏の失業率とインフレ率～経済理論から見る統計データ～

Source PDF: all report pdf/フィリップス曲線からみるユーロ圏の失業率とインフレ率～経済理論から見る統計データ～.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

経済学の教科書や経済書、ビジネス書には、さまざまなマクロ経済学の用語や理論が登場する。経
済の動きを理解するうえで、こうした理論は重要な手がかりとなる。一方で、実際の経済はさまざま
な要因によって動いている。このコラムでは、マクロ経済学を学ぶ初学者や、経済について興味を持
つ社会人、学生を対象に、マクロ経済学の代表的な用語や理論について、その基本的な考え方を整理
するとともに、実際のデータを基に経済でどのような動きがみられるのかを確認する。 
本稿では「フィリップス曲線」を取り上げる。ユーロ圏の統計データを用いて、失業率とインフレ
率の間にトレードオフの関係が見られるのかを確認する。

「失業率が低いときにはインフレ率が高くなりやすく、反対に、失業率が高いときにはインフレ率
が低くなりやすい」という失業率とインフレ率の短期的なトレードオフの関係を表したものが「フィ
リップス曲線（Phillips curve） 」である。ニュージーランド出身の経済学者アルバン・ウィリアム・
フィリップス（Alban William Housego Phillips）は、1861 年から 1957 年までの英国の統計を分析
し、失業率が低いときほど賃金上昇率が高く、失業率が高いときほど賃金上昇率が低いという関係を
明らかにした。その後、賃金上昇率と物価上昇率（インフレ率）に相関関係があることから、フィリ
ップス曲線は、失業率とインフレ率の関係を表すものとして広く知られるようになった。では、なぜ
失業率とインフレ率の間に、このような関係が生じるのだろうか。景気が拡大し、モノやサービスへ
の需要が増えると、企業は生産を拡大するために採用を増やす。その結果、雇用が増え、失業率は低
下する。失業率が低いということは、働く意思のある人の多くがすでに仕事に就いており、企業にと
って新たな人材を確保しにくい状態でもある。人手不足が強まれば、企業は人材を確保するために賃
金を引き上げるようになる。賃金上昇は家計の所得を増やし、消費を押し上げる一方、企業にとって 
は人件費の増加につながる。需要の拡大や人件費の上昇を背景に企業がモノやサービスの価格を引き
上げれば、 物価全体にも上昇圧力がかかる。 このように、 景気の拡大に伴って失業率が低下する一方、
賃金や物価には上昇圧力がかかる。 フィリップス曲線は、 一般に 「インフレ率」 を縦軸、 「失業率」 を
横軸に取り、右下がりの曲線として描かれる（図①）。 
2026-10-09 
研究員 
の眼 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 
フィリップス曲線からみるユーロ
圏の失業率とインフレ率 
～経済理論から見る統計データ～

### Page 2

（図表1）

（図表2）

図②は、2006 年以降のユーロ圏における失業率とインフレ率の関係を示したものである。 データ全
体をみると、失業率とインフレ率の間にはおおむね右下がりの傾向がみられる（図②赤線）。ただし、
その関係は時期によって異なっている。そこで以下では、時期ごとの動きに着目し、フィリップス曲
線が示す関係が実際にどのようにみられたのかを確認してみる。 
まず、フィリップス曲線が示す関係と比較的整合的な動きがみられたのが、世界金融危機前後の
2008～09 年（図②青）である。2008 年3 月に7.3％だった失業率は、その後上昇し、2009 年10 月に
は 10.1％に達した。一方、インフレ率は、2008 年 3 月の3.6％から2009 年 10 月には▲0.1％まで低
下した。つまり、この時期には、失業率が上昇する一方でインフレ率が低下するという、フィリップ
ス曲線が示す関係と整合的な動きがみられた。 
また 2016 年以降（図②緑）には、失業率の低下とともにインフレ率が上昇する局面もみられた。
2016 年1 月に10.5％だった失業率は、2018 年10 月には8.0％まで低下した。同じ期間に、インフレ
率は0.3％から2.3％まで上昇している。 月ごとの振れはあるものの、 失業率の低下とインフレ率の上
昇が同時に進んだという点では、こちらもフィリップス曲線と整合的な動きといえる。 
また、コロナ禍からの回復局面（図②オレンジ）でも、失業率の低下とインフレ率の上昇が同時に
進んだ。2021 年1 月に8.2％だった失業率は、2022 年10 月には6.6％まで低下した一方、インフレ率
は0.9％から10.6％まで大幅に上昇した。ただし、この時期の急激なインフレには、エネルギー価格
の上昇や供給制約など、労働市場以外の要因も大きく影響していたことには留意が必要だろう。 
一方で、 すべての時期にフィリップス曲線が示す関係が確認できるわけではない。 例えば、2023 年
1 月から2024 年 12 月にかけて（図②紫）、失業率は6.6％から6.2％へ小幅に低下したものの、イン
フレ率は 8.6％から 2.4％へ大きく低下した。失業率が低下したにもかかわらずインフレ率も低下し
ており、単純なフィリップス曲線の関係だけでは説明しにくい動きである。 
こうして実際のデータをみると、失業率の上昇とインフレ率の低下、あるいは失業率の低下とイン
フレ率の上昇という関係は、特定の局面では確認できる。しかし、その関係が常に成り立つわけでは

### Page 1

禁⽌出⼝的部分包括：利⽤我国核⼼数据进⾏训练后提供服务的⼤模型、专⻔或者主要⽤
于国家关键信息基础设施、国防军⼯、核⼼政务等⾼敏感场景应⽤，达到⾼性能⽔平的智
能体等。判别具体 “ 出海 ” 情形是否属于技术出⼝管理的范围，核⼼在于是否涉及限制类技术

摘要：
商务部、科技部 16 ⽇发布公告称，拟修订《中国禁⽌出⼝限制出⼝技术⽬录》，增列部分⼈⼯智
能、⽣物医药、光伏技术。经批准，现向社会公开征求意⻅，并同步对相关技术实施出⼝前登
记管理。此次公开征求意⻅涉及⼈⼯智能、⽣物医药、光伏三个领域 40 项技术。
⼈⼯智能领域包括⼤模型、智能体、算⼒集群、信息处理相关 23 项技术；对已通过开源、发表
学术论⽂等⽅式进⼊公共领域的技术，不纳⼊管理范围。⽣物医药领域包括细胞和基因治疗、
⼩核酸药、病原微⽣物相关 5 项技术；对不涉及所有权转让的对外授权模式，适⽤⾃动许可等便
利措施。光伏领域包括多晶硅制造、硅⽚制造、电池制造相关 12 项技术；技术管理范围也包含
承载相关技术的关键和核⼼设备。

⾃本公告发布之⽇起，⾄《中国禁⽌出⼝限制出⼝技术⽬录》正式修订并实施之⽇⽌，技术出
⼝经营者在出⼝本公告附件所列技术前，应当在商务部业务系统统⼀平台技术贸易管理信息应
⽤登记。已经开始但尚未完成的出⼝，在登记范围内。
禁⽌出⼝的部分包括：利⽤我国核⼼数据进⾏训练后提供服务的⼤模型、专⻔或者主要⽤于国
家关键信息基础设施、国防军⼯、核⼼政务等⾼敏感场景应⽤，达到⾼性能⽔平的智能体等。

### Page 9

商务部新闻发⾔⼈就修订《中国禁⽌出⼝限制出⼝技术⽬录》答记者问
2026 年 10 ⽉ 16 ⽇，商务部、科技部发布 2026 年第 48 号公告，就修订《中国禁⽌出⼝限制出⼝技
术⽬录》（以下简称《⽬录》）公开征求意⻅，并对相关技术实施出⼝前登记的临时管理措施。⽇
前，商务部新闻发⾔⼈就有关问题回答了记者提问。
问：《⽬录》修订的背景是什么？
答：根据《中华⼈⺠共和国对外贸易法》第⼆⼗条，国务院对外贸易主管部⻔会同国务院其他有
关部⻔，制定、调整并发布禁⽌或限制出⼝的技术⽬录。⾃ 2001 年以来，商务部会同科技部多
次调整修订《⽬录》，有进有出，有管有放，已形成⼯作惯例。此次修订亦是技术贸易管理的常
态化⼯作，依法依规进⾏，不针对任何特定国家、地区或实体。
近年来，⼈⼯智能、⽣物医药和光伏相关技术在敏感领域的应⽤不断拓展，各主要经济体均实施
了不同程度的出⼝管控。中国参考国际通⾏做法，依法实施必要的管理，既防范技术失控和不当
扩散⻛险，也保障正常国际技术交流合作，推动科技向上向善、安全可控、造福⼈类。

问：《⽬录》拟新增技术的范围有哪些？
答：此次公开征求意⻅涉及拟增列的⼈⼯智能、⽣物医药、光伏三个领域 40 项关键技术。其
中，拟禁⽌出⼝技术 14 项，包括：⼈⼯智能驱动的恶意代码⽣成与变异技术、具有⾃主知识产
权的细胞和基因治疗技术⼯艺平台、钙钛矿及叠层电池组件⽣产技术等；拟限制出⼝技术 26
项，包括：⼈⼯智能辅助漏洞挖掘技术、具有⾃主知识产权的通⽤型细胞和基因治疗全新靶点发
现和验证技术、单线产能 ≥3 万吨 / 年的多晶硅⼯程技术等。公告还明确了适⽤便利措施等情形。
详情请参考公告原⽂。
问：《⽬录》修订是否充分考虑产业发展情况？

### Page 10

答：中国政府⼀贯⽀持企业依法依规开展互利共赢的跨国经营与国际技术合作，有关政策⽴场没
有改变。针对此次公开征求意⻅的技术范围和政策，商务部、科技部会同⾏业主管部⻔，事前已
⼴泛组织研究机构、⾏业组织开展深⼊评估，并充分听取主要企业的意⻅建议，充分考虑国内外
产业现状和发展需求。具体表现在三个⽅⾯：⼀是列管范围精准有限。此次拟列管的多数技术已
明确具体技术指标，且利⽤相关技术制造的产品不在管理范围内，管理范围清晰、精准、有限，
为企业划定了合规边界，便于政策执⾏。⼆是适⽤便利措施。此次列管明确了拟适⽤便利措施的
多种出⼝情形，体现了分级分类的管理理念，有助于增强政策的透明度和可预期性，确保技术出
⼝安全、规范、有序。三是实施动态调整。商务部、科技部将依法对《⽬录》管理范围实施动态
调整，根据技术发展情况，及时将不先进或落后技术移出《⽬录》。此次征求意⻅是在更⼤范围
内听取各⽅意⻅建议的⼯作安排。
问：对于拟增列的⼈⼯智能技术，主要有哪些考虑？
答：当前，全球⼈⼯智能技术创新进⼊前所未有的活跃期，机遇与挑战并存。此次拟将⼈⼯智能

⼤模型、智能体、算⼒集群、信息处理相关 23 项关键技术增列⾄《⽬录》，主要有以下考虑：
⼀是确保⼈⼯智能技术安全可控。⼈⼯智能引发的各类内⽣和衍⽣⻛险已引起全球各国的⾼度关
注。此次拟列管的⼈⼯智能⾃主驱动恶意代码⽣成与变异技术等，可被⽤于攻击国家关键信息基
础设施、国防军⼯、核⼼政务等敏感系统，或⽤于绕过国家安全防御体系。若被⾮国家⾏为体或
恶意实体获取，可能对国家安全和⼈类共同安全造成危害。中国政府对这类技术实施必要管理，
⽬的是筑牢安全底线，防范滥⽤恶⽤，确保⼈⼯智能始终处于⼈类控制之下，推进⼈⼯智能国际
治理。
⼆是⿎励开源开放、合作共享。中国政府致⼒于让⼈⼯智能成为促进共同繁荣、维护共同安全的
⼀个重要动⼒源。为⽀持构建⼈⼯智能开源创新⽣态体系，对⼈⼯智能类技术开源、中国企业在
境外⾃建⾃营算⼒中⼼、赴境外开展芯⽚流⽚制造、利⽤境外资源开展联合研发、参与国际标准
制定等情形的技术出⼝，适⽤⾃动许可等便利措施，基本实现 “ 即报即出 ” ；对已通过开源、发表
学术论⽂等⽅式进⼊公共领域的相关技术，拟不纳⼊出⼝管理范围，为⼈⼯智能技术国际交流合

作创造良好环境。
需要特别指出的是，中⽅主张在相互尊重、平等互利基础上，与各国开展⼈⼯智能对话合作，共
同应对⼈⼯智能技术扩散和失控⻛险。中⽅⽀持全球南⽅国家加强⼈⼯智能能⼒建设，弥合数智
鸿沟，促进可持续发展。对于世界⼈⼯智能合作组织成员间开展能⼒建设国际合作、技术共享和
规则共建所需的技术出⼝，将提供更多便利和⽀持。
问：《⽬录》正式实施后，⼈⼯智能⼤模型在海外本地化部署、向境外提供模型调⽤等各类 “ 出
海 ” 形式，是否属于技术出⼝管理范围？
答：当前，⼈⼯智能⼤模型和应⽤产品的 “ 出海 ” 模式多种多样，判别具体 “ 出海 ” 情形是否属于技
术出⼝管理的范围，核⼼在于是否涉及限制类技术从中国境内向境外转移。
此次纳⼊监管的⼈⼯智能技术范围精准有限，有关措施正式实施后，绝⼤部分⼈⼯智能技术仍可
以⾃由出⼝。对于纳⼊限制类的⼈⼯智能技术，开展海外本地化部署，属于技术出⼝管理范围；
如果部署在海外⾃有云等⻛险相对可控的情形下，可适⽤⾃动许可等便利措施。对于企业利⽤限
制类⼤模型对外提供 API 调⽤、 APP/ ⽹⻚交互、 SaaS 应⽤、本地端侧轻量化部署等情形，不属

于⼤模型技术出⼝管理范围。
问：对于拟增列的⽣物医药技术，主要有哪些考虑？
答：现⾏《⽬录》中已有部分⽣物医药技术，此次拟增列病原微⽣物、细胞和基因治疗、⼩核酸
药相关 5 项技术，主要有以下考虑：

### Page 11

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
⼀是维护⼈类⽣命健康安全。病原微⽣物技术等直接关系⼈类健康和国家⽣物安全，将其纳⼊
《⽬录》实施禁⽌出⼝，是维护国家安全、构筑⽣物安全屏障的制度安排，也是保护全⼈类健康
的客观需要。
⼆是⽀持我国⽣物医药技术依法依规有序开展国际合作。此次列管⽣物医药技术，对国际合作的
重要情形作出排除或适⽤便利措施。例如，对具有⾃主知识产权的相关技术⼯艺平台，对外授权
许可、利⽤相关技术⼯艺提供外包研发和⽣产服务等出⼝情形，不纳⼊管理范围；对于具有⾃主
知识产权的相关靶点发现和验证技术，对外授权许可⽅式出⼝的，适⽤⾃动许可等便利措施。
三是践⾏⼈类卫⽣健康共同体理念。此次政策明确规定，对于限制出⼝技术，因公共卫⽣突发事
件、⼈道主义救援、利⽤境外资源开展联合研发等需要的出⼝，适⽤⾃动许可等便利措施。中国
将继续与世界各国共享技术发展成果，为增进⼈类健康福祉作出积极贡献。

问：对于拟增列的光伏技术，主要有哪些考虑？
答：此次拟将多晶硅制造、硅⽚制造、电池制造等环节的 12 项光伏技术增列⾄《⽬录》，主要有
以下考虑：
⼀是维护国家安全和利益。近年来，光伏技术在航天、国防、⽆⼈装备、⾼安全等级基础设施等
敏感领域的应⽤不断拓展，光伏技术出⼝可能引致的国家安全⻛险不断上升，有必要及时适度实
施监管。
⼆是⽀持绿⾊技术共享。此次列管技术范围有限，其他未达到管控指标的技术仍可⾃由出⼝。公
告中列明少量光伏关键设备，仅针对承载达到管控指标技术的情形。未列明的其他光伏设备，原
则上不适⽤本公告要求。
三是完善技术出⼝监管制度。光伏关键技术的出⼝往往伴随光伏产线的出⼝、安装、调试、改良
和升级⽽发⽣。出⼝经营者从事上述活动的，应按规定申请技术出⼝许可证，并凭证办理外汇、
银⾏、税务、海关等相关⼿续。
问：对拟增列的技术实施出⼝前登记管理的主要考虑和具体要求是什么？
答：为掌握有关技术出⼝需求情况，提升科学管理⽔平，并防范潜在出⼝⻛险，根据《中华⼈⺠
共和国对外贸易法》第⼗⼋条、第⼆⼗条有关规定，经批准，⾃商务部、科技部 2026 年第 48 号

公告发布之⽇起，⾄《中国禁⽌出⼝限制出⼝技术⽬录》正式修订公告实施之⽇⽌，技术出⼝经
营者在出⼝公告所列技术前，应当在商务部业务系统统⼀平台技术贸易管理信息应⽤登记。出⼝
经营者应⾃⾏判断拟出⼝技术是否落⼊临时登记管理范围。已经开始但尚未完成的出⼝，在登记
范围内。需登记的出⼝情形包括：向境外转让技术所有权，境内技术授权境外使⽤，境内技术的
所有权⼈或者实际控制⼈变更为境外主体，以及商务部明确要求登记的其他情形。需要特别说明
的是，对于列⼊《⽬录》管理范围的光伏关键和核⼼设备出⼝，均需按照规定登记。企业在登记
中遇到问题和困难，可以及时向商务部、科技部咨询。

Document 475: 利上げで悪化する一部AI関連企業の資金調達環境 ～半導体市場に影響も、中長期的な市場拡大は変わらず～

Source PDF: all report pdf/利上げで悪化する一部AI関連企業の資金調達環境 ～半導体市場に影響も、中長期的な市場拡大は変わらず～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Investment Navigator

2026 年 10 月 16 日

1/4 
利上げで悪化する一部 AI 関連企業の資金調達環境 
～半導体市場に影響も、中長期的な市場拡大は変わらず～ 
 経済調査部 シニア・フェロー  嶌峰 義清 
（℡：050-5474-7489）

（要旨） 
○ FRB が9 月に3 年 2 カ月ぶりの利上げに踏み切り、 政策金利が中立金利の水準に近づくなか、
AI 関連企業の資金調達環境に変化が出てきた。 データセンターを借入で建設するネオクラウ
ド企業の社債利回りは15%近くまで上昇し、市場では信用不安の様相も見られる。 
○ 韓国の出荷・在庫バランスは、半導体、電子部品ともに悪化した。台湾でも電子・光学産業
の受注の勢いが鈍り、顧客在庫が「過剰」に転じるなど、需給が変調する兆しが出ている。 
○ それでも、 ハイパースケーラーの設備投資やメモリーの供給不足をみる限り、AI 関連需要の
拡大は中長期的に続く可能性が高い。今後は「AI ならば何でも買われる」局面から、財務の
健全性と需給をもとに銘柄が選別される局面に移ると考えられる。

1.  利上げが中立水準に届き、市場の前提が変わる 
FRBは9 月のFOMCで、政策金利の誘導目標を0.25％引き上げ、3.75～4.00％とした（図表1）。
利上げの背景にあるのは、根強いイ
ンフレだ。9 月の消費者物価指数は前
年比＋3.7％と市場予想を上回った。
市場では10 月27～28 日のFOMCで追
加利上げがあるとの見方が再び強ま
りつつある。金利の上昇は国債市場
でも顕著で、2 年債利回りは5％に迫
り、10 年債利回りも5.3％台まで上
昇している。 
FRBは、長期的に望ましい政策金利
（景気を刺激も抑制もしない中立金
利）を 3.25～3.50％とみている。足
元の政策金利はすでにその水準を超
えており、金融政策は景気を抑制す
注：色つき部分はFRBによる中立金利水準域
出所：FRB
（図表1）米国の政策金利（FF金利）の推移
0.0
0.5
1.0
1.5
2.0
2.5
3.0
3.5
4.0
4.5

（％）

### Page 2

2/4 
る領域に入っている。金融引き締めが実体経済に効いてくるまでには通常1～2 年かかる。それで
も、景気の先行きを読む金融市場は、早い段階で影響を受けやすい。 
なかでも金利上昇の影響を受けやすいのが、高い成長期待を背景に株価が形成されてきたAI・
半導体関連株だ。将来の利益への期待が大きい企業ほど、割引率が上がると理論的な株価の下落
幅が大きくなる。加えて今回は、金利上昇が株価評価の調整にとどまらず、AI投資そのものを支
える資金調達の経路にまで影響し始めた点に注意が必要だ。

2.  データセンター投資企業の社債利回りが急騰 
その象徴となったのが、AI向けクラウド事業者であるCoreWeaveの社債だ。10 月14 日、同社が
発行した2032 年満期債は14.8％程度まで上昇し、株価は1 日で15％超下落した。同日には
Oracleの株価も9％近く下落した。これを受けて、10 月 15 日の日経平均株価は前日比▲3318.34
円（▲4.9％）と、過去3 番目となる下落幅を記録した。指数への寄与が大きい半導体関連株の下
げが主導したもので、日経半導体株指数は同▲10.1％と7 月 28 日以来の下落率を記録している。 
10 月14 日のCoreWeave社の 2032 年満期債（利率6.25％）の利回りは7.24％まで上昇した。ハ
イパースケーラー（大手クラウド事業者）の大量の社債発行が続くなか、9 月下旬時点で、AI関連
の発行体の社債スプレッドは約115bp（Goldman Sachs集計）に広がった。一方、投資適格社債全
体は 83bp（ICE BofA）と、年初からほぼ横ばいにとどまっている。つまり、社債市場全体の信用
環境は比較的安定しているが、AI関連の発行体だけが選別され、より高い上乗せ金利を求められ
ている。 
背景にあるのは、社債の供給量の多さだ。Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Oracleの 4 社による社債
発行額は、10 月13 日時点で2,280 億ドルを超えた。これは2025 年の年間発行額の2 倍以上にあ
たる。2027 年にはハイパースケーラー全体の発行額が4,200 億ドルと、2026 年を6 割上回るとの
予想もある。投資対象が同業種の社債に偏ることを回避するため「AI以外の社債を求める」動き
も目立ち始めた。実際、9 月のAI関連の社債発行額は8 月の半分以下に落ち込んだ。 
こうした変化は、AI投資の資金調達に「量」と「価格」の両面から制約が生じ始めたことを示
している。巨額の手元資金を持つ大手ハイパースケーラーにとって、利回りの上昇は直ちに投資
を抑える要因にはならないだろう。一方、借入に頼ってデータセンターを建設するネオクラウド
企業は事情が異なる。資金調達コストの上昇は、事業計画の見直しにつながりやすい。AI投資の
「周辺部」から設備投資のペースが鈍れば、それは半導体需要の伸びの鈍化として波及していく
ことになる。

3.  韓国・台湾で出荷・在庫バランスに変調 
実際、半導体の需給にも変化の兆しが出ている。韓国の8 月の鉱工業生産統計で前年比をみる
と、半導体は出荷の伸びが＋2.5％にとどまる一方、在庫は＋8.8％増加した。出荷の伸びから在
庫の伸びを差し引いた出荷・在庫バランスは▲6.3 ポイントと、在庫の積み上がりが出荷を上回っ
ている。電子部品はさらに悪化が鮮明だ。出荷が前年比▲5.1％減少する一方、在庫は＋16.2％増

### Page 3

3/4 
加し、出荷・在庫バランスは▲21.3 ポイントまで落ち込んだ。台湾の電子部品の出荷・在庫バラ
ンスも▲13.0 ポイント（7 月）とマイナス圏に沈んでいる（図表2）。 
韓国の8 月の半導体輸出額は前年比3 倍を超え、過去最高を更新した。それにもかかわらず出
荷の伸びが2％台にとどまっていることは、輸出額の急増の多くが、メモリーをはじめとする価格
の上昇によってもたらされていることを示唆している。数量の面では、需要の勢いはすでに鈍り
始めている可能性がある。 
台湾でも同様の兆しがある。8 月の電子部品の生産指数は過去最高を更新したが、サーバー製品
の仕様変更や生産計画の調整を受け、伸びは政府の予想を下回った。より先行的な指標である9
月の製造業PMIを電子・光学産業についてみると、次のような変化が出ている。 
✓ 新規受注指数が10 ポイント以上低下 
✓ 受注残指数も10 ポイント以上下がり、54.3％と2025 年 12 月以来の低い水準になった 
✓ 在庫指数は64.8％へ上昇 
✓ 顧客在庫指数は51.0％と、2022 年12 月以来の高い水準で「過剰」に転じた 
一部の部品だけが不足し、ほかの部品が余るミスマッチも広がっている。そのため、受注と生
産がともに急拡大していた局面から、高水準ながら受注の勢いが鈍り、在庫の負担が増す局面へ
と移りつつある。 
この変調の背景には、AIの外側の需要が弱っていることもある。メモリー価格の急騰は、スマ
ートフォンやパソコンなど消費者向け電子機器のコストを押し上げている。スマートフォンなど
では値上げも相次いでいる。値上げによって買い替えの周期が長くなれば、AI以外の用途の半導
体や電子部品の需要が弱まり、在庫調整の動きが広がる可能性がある。

4.  それでも中長期のAI需要拡大は続く 
もっとも、こうした兆しをもって、AI関連需要の拡大が終わったと判断するのは早計だろう。 
※出荷在庫バランスは［出荷（前年比）－在庫（前年比）］
出所：Datastream

韓国(半導体) 台湾(電子部品)
（％）

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
第一に、需要の中心であるハイパースケーラーの投資意欲は依然として強い。ハイパースケー
ラーの設備投資額は2026 年に 8,000 億ドル規模に達し、2027 年には1 兆ドルを超えるとの見方が
出ている。大手5 社の設備投資の米国GDPに対する比率は、2026 年の2.4％から 2027 年には3.2％
に上昇すると見込まれる。ITバブル期（90 年代後半から2000 年頃）のインターネット網整備（ピ
ーク時で1.2％）をはるかに上回る規模だ。 
第二に、供給側の制約は依然として強い。サムスン電子とSKハイニックスのメモリー在庫は10
日分を下回ったとの推計もあり、米マイクロンは2028 年度まで需給の逼迫が続くとの見方を示し
ている。台湾のTSMCの9 月の売上高は前年比54.6％増と、9 月として過去最高を記録した。エヌ
ビディアも次の四半期に1,080 億ドルの売上高を見込んでいる。韓国の半導体在庫の増加には、
休暇シーズンや生産計画の調整に伴う一時的な面も含まれている。月次の統計の振れも大きいた
め、1～2 カ月のデータだけで循環的な調整局面入りと結論づけることはできない。 
一方で、投資の規模が大きくなるにつれ、それを支える資金面での余裕は薄くなっている。大
手5 社でさえ、2027 年から2028 年にかけてはAI投資の支出が営業キャッシュフローを上回ると見
込まれている。営業キャッシュフローがプラスに転じるのは2029 年と予想されている。つまりAI
投資は、手元資金だけで賄える段階から、外部からの資金調達に頼る段階に入りつつある。金利
上昇と社債市場での選別が強まれば、投資計画が「中止」されることはなくても、その「時期」
や「ペース」が見直される可能性は否定できない。 
以上を踏まえると、AI・半導体株を取り巻く環境は、成長期待だけで株価が上昇してきた局面
から、次の三つの要素を同時に見極めなければならない局面に移りつつあるといえる。 
① 金融政策が引き締め方向に転じたことによる株価評価の調整 
② 資金調達コストの上昇による投資ペースの鈍化 
③ 出荷・在庫バランスの悪化に表れる需給の変調 
利上げを実施すれば2 会合連続となる10 月末のFOMCは、市場の方向感を左右する可能性があ
る。利上げを実施すれば、FRBは多少の景気減速を厭わない姿勢を見せることに繋がるため、市場
の警戒感は一気に高まろう。社債市場においては、格付け感のスプレッドが更に拡大すると見込
まれ、特にAI関連ではその格差が一層大きくなることが懸念される。 
ただし、AIが中長期の成長分野であるという見方そのものが揺らいでいるわけではない。今後
は、同じAI関連の企業の間でも差がつきやすくなるだろう。厚い手元資金を持つ大手ハイパース
ケーラーや、供給不足が続くメモリー・先端ロジック・先端パッケージは、相対的に底堅い動き
が期待できる。一方で、借入に頼って投資を拡大してきた企業や、AI以外の用途への依存度が高
い電子部品分野は、調整の圧力を受けやすい。 
半導体関連の比重が高い日本株、とりわけ日経平均株価にとっても、こうした選別の動きは無
縁ではない。中長期の拡大基調を前提としつつも、短期的には在庫調整と株価評価の調整が重な
る可能性に留意する必要があるだろう。

以 上

⚫ 2Q26 財報 EPS 優 於 預 期 、2H26 逐 季 顯 著 季 增 趨 勢 明 確 
⚫ 設 備 產 能 持 續 翻 倍 ， 搭 配 ASP 同 步 成 長 挹 注 獲 利 倍 增 
⚫ TGV -ICP 預估 2H28 起 加 速 放 量 ， 並 為 中 長 期 營 運 支 柱 
2Q26 毛利率小幅遜於預期 ：創新服務 2Q26 營收 3.6 億元； 毛利率79.1%
受制 日本原料 商交期變長 導致整體出貨遞延 ，故小幅遜於預期 ；惟在營
收規模效益加持下營益率 達 45.4% 、EPS  3.4 元，均仍優於前手預期。   
2H26 探 針 卡 整 線 設 備 出 貨 加 速 ，預 期 營 運 逐 季 顯 著 成 長： 觀 察 公 司
2Q26 存貨中原料、在製品 均已顯著回升 ，我們認為 後續 出貨速度將有
所改善 ，預期 每月 平均 出貨 8-9 台， 挹注 2H26 營運顯著季增 ；且因 業
務貢獻 以雙臂 式 MEMS 植針機為主 ，故亦看好 2H26 毛利率維持 80%+。 
探針卡客戶 擴產 腳步 積極，對應公司 2026 -2028 年 逐年翻倍擴產展望 ：
我們 預期國際 探針卡客戶 規劃 2027 年將擴產至 2,000 萬針 ，為當前產
能近 3 倍規模； 公司 作為專屬植針機設備供應商，目前亦加速於台中二
/三廠備戰產能，我們更預期在 此客戶 強勁展望下，公司 2026/2027/2028
年雙臂植針機擴產展望分別達 75/150/300 台，且因設備代數持續升級
刺激 ASP 成長 、 及部分配備 AOI 檢測的非 MEMS 植針機台單價亦相對
優惠，故預估 2026/2027/2028 年設備營收佔比仍達 90%/85%/75% 。 
2027 年預期植針代工為主 ，材料包 /銅柱模組 2H27 接續 貢獻 ： 參考客
戶導入現況，我們預期 2026/2027 年 除 設 備 外主 要 貢 獻 皆 來 自 植 針 代
工，且因其毛利率更勝材料包，有利毛利率展望維持高檔；不過 2H27
起隨客戶 需求加持 ，預估 材料包及銅柱模組 將接 棒貢獻 ；此外，我們發
現公司 銅柱模組 技術在 CPO 應用具採用價值， 為未來值得 追蹤 之處 。 
持續提前準備 TGV-ICP 產能，預估 2H28 逐步放量： 我們現依大廠晶
片藍圖進程，調整 TGV-ICP 放量時間點至 2H28 ，但因客戶需求龐大 可
期，故預期 2029 -2030 年公司相關產能仍有望快速滿載， 並成為 2028
年後營運成長主力 ，而公司也 須於 2027 年起便備足產能因應客戶所需 。 
產 業 趨 勢 明 確 且 成 長 爆 發 力 具 延 續 性 ， 給 予 「 買 進 」 評 等： 我們 預估
2026/2027/2028 年 EPS 為 16. 7、46.4 、110.3 元， 考量在產業趨勢明確
下公司展望爆發力同具延續性， 對高評價亦具支持，故給予 「買進」建
議，目標價為 2,730 元(2H27 -1H28 EPS*4 0 倍 PER) 。 
風險因子： 產能擴充 速度 不如預期 、TGV-ICP 量產 時程 延後 。

公司介紹 ：創新服務成立於 2004 年，截至 9M26 員工人數為 213 人，公司 據
點 涵蓋 新 竹、台中 總 廠(興 安 、中 山， 並做 為主要 生 產基 地)及 高雄 辦 公室；
目前產品別包含探針卡整線設備自動化方案 (以 MEMS 探針卡雙臂 式植針機
為主軸 )、半導體精密加工 (如返修設備、碰焊機 …等)、與先進封裝解決方案
(如銅柱模組、 TGV-ICP 等，見圖表一 )，另預期在 2027 年，材料包 /植針代
工 業 務 也 將 加 速 放 量 。 公 司 現 今 絕 大 多 數 產 能 即 服 務 探 針 卡 大廠
Technoprobe(TPRO.MI) ，除做為其專屬設備供應商外 (亦即 設備均是 直接出貨
給 Technoprobe) ，現亦持續拓展客戶群至 Technoprobe 集團合作業者 。
